2. A.     02.25.20 
2. RECOGNITION 
  A. Introduction of New Board Member 
   We would like to take th is opportunity to introduce and welcome our newly 
appointed Board trustee, Mrs. Vital Anne.  Mrs. Anne was officially sworn in 
just before our meeting this evening.  Vital and her husband, Siva, are 
originally from India and have been residents of Troy for 16 years.  They 
have two daughters – one is a graduate of the International Academy -East 
and the other is a junior at Athens.  Vital is an engineering  and STEM 
professional with more than 25 years’ experience in various roles from Project 
Engineer to Vice President. She is also a tireless volunteer in our schools, 
serving as coach of Science Olympiad teams and in key positions on PTOs, 
Athletic and Music Boosters as well as sports and extracurricular activities at 
multiple schools. In the greater Troy and Metro Detroit community, Vital gives 
of her time and talents as a tutor in Detroit Public Schools and as a volunteer 
and fundraiser for the MS Society. Vital has a Master’s degree in mechanical 
engineering and is passionate about children, education, STEM, and 
green/renewable energy.  She loves watching sports – especially football and 
soccer and enjoys Indian classical dance.   Please join us in welcoming Mrs. 
Anne to our oneTROY family.

 2. C.       02.25.20 
2. RECOGNITION 
C. TSD Mission Moment – MASTA 2020 Administrator of the  
       Year – Ryan Brinks 
This evening, the Board would like to recognize Ryan Brinks, Associate Principal 
of International Academy-East, who received the 2020 Michigan ASTA (American 
String Teachers Association) Administrator of the Year award.  The award is 
given annually to an administrator who has made a significant contribution to 
the promotion, maintenance, and/or growth of string education and the music 
program.  Mr. Brinks w as honored with this award at the Michigan Music 
Conference in Grand Rapids this past  January.  At this time, Mrs. Christine 
DiPilato, Assistant Superintendent, Secondary Instruction, will share a bit more 
about Ryan’s nomination for this award.   
Recommended Action:  Invite Christine DiPilato to the podium then  
ask Ryan to come up and receive congratulations 
from the Board.

                                  2. D.     02.25.20 
2.       RECOGNITION 
D.  Principal Appreciation Month 
Throughout the State of Michigan, February has traditionally been celebrated as 
Principal Appreciation Month, in recognition of the outstanding work of our 
building-level educational leaders. In Troy, we are fortunate to have an exemplary 
group of principals dedicated to the success of every student in the District. This 
evening, we will pass a resolution to commend our elementary and secondary 
principals for their exceptional commitment to excellence in the Troy School 
District.  
WHEREAS, energetic and inspiring school leadership is essential if teachers, 
schools and districts are to ensure that all Troy students are college  and career 
ready; and, 
WHEREAS, K-12 school principals play a vital role in the success of students and 
act as the liaison between the school and the community it serves, ensuring that 
parents and the community are aware of student and school achievements; and, 
WHEREAS, Troy School District principals are dedicated,  quality educational 
professionals committed to providing sound leadership and guidance to their staff 
and students; and,  
WHEREAS, during this month, we join with educators, parents and students 
throughout Michigan to raise awareness of the importance of educational 
leadership, and to recognize and thank all principals working in Troy for their 
exemplary service; 
NOW THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED , that the Troy Board of Education 
officially recognizes February as Principal Appreciation Month in Troy and thanks 
our dedicated building leaders for all they do for our students and staff.    
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Read and adopt resolution.

  Supported by:

RECOGNITION 
TSD Mission Moment – Michigan 
Arctic Grayling Initiative  
A s  t h i s  e v e n i n g ’ s  M i s s i o n  M o m e n t ,  t h e  B o a r d  
recognized Athens TV students and their teacher Adam 
Burns  for  researching  the  extinction  of  the  Arctic 
Grayling fish in Michigan waters.  After learning about 
the  Michigan  Arctic  Grayling  Initiative  (MAGI),  the 
students began working on a documentary to use as a 
promotional  piece  to  get  people  involved  in  helping 
return this native species to its home.  The have invited 
students  from  environmental  science  classes  to  meet 
w i t h  r e s e a r c h e r s  t h e y  h a v e  i n t e r v i e w e d  a n d  t h e  
experts they have met to foster relationships that could 
lead  to  authentic  learning  opportunities  for  in  the 
future. They have encouraged classes to apply for the 
DNR's  Salmon  In  The  Classroom  project,  where 
students  can  grow  salmon  in  classroom  tanks  and 
release into the wild.  Their goal is to finish the film by 
mid‐February  and  hope  to  visit  Alaska  to  capture  the 
important step of gathering the Arctic Grayling in the 
field.  Students  
Kris Griffor – 2020 Excellence in 
Griffor,  Assistant  Superintendent,  Elementary 
Instruction for receiving the 2020 National Excellence in 
Literacy  Leadership  Award.    This  is  an  award  that  is 
presented  by  the  Reading  Recovery  Teacher  Leaders 
across  the  country  to  an  administrator  that  has 
displayed  a  strong  commitment  to  expand  and 
maintain the high standards of Reading Recovery and 
made  significant  contributions  to  implementation 
beyond the local level.   
  7 January 21, 2020 
15
Schedule for 2020 Calendar Year 
20‐010 
It  was  moved  by  Dr.  Philippart  and  supported  by  Mr. 
Hauff  that BE  IT  THEREFORE  RESOLVED  that  the  Troy 
Literacy Leadership Award Winner 
This  evening,  the  Board  would  like  to  recognize  Kris 
celebrates  the  work  of  a  very  important  group  of 
individuals: The Board of Education.  
Our trustees help us to plant the seeds of education in 
our  students  and  support  the  very  important  work  of 
our  teachers  and  staff  as  we  nurture  and  feed  young 
minds. Sometimes the fruits of that labor aren’t visible 
for years to come, but we know that they are growing 
under  the  surface,  building  the  skills  students  need: 
critical  thinking,  citizenship,  communication, 
collaboration, character and creativity. 
S o  t o n i g h t ,  a s  a  t o k e n  o f  o u r  a p p r e c i a t i o n  f o r  y o u r  
tireless efforts on behalf of our students and staff, we 
present  you  with  these  planter  boxes.  May  the 
succulents  inside  serve  as  a  reminder  of  the  students 
you impact, their thirst for knowledge and their natural 
curiosity  about  the  world  around  them.  The  soil 
represents  the  environment  that  you  help  create,  a 
habitat  rich  with  the  support  each  student  needs  to 
grow  and  thrive.  Finally,  the  box  itself  symbolizes  the 
strong foundation that you, as trustees, help to build for 
today’s students and generations to come. 
We hope that this small gift will signify to you how much 
we value your role in making the Troy School District a 
World Class system in which to grow and learn.

 7. B.   02.25.20 
7. CURRICULUM 
B.  CONSIDERATION:   Schools Closed to Open Enrollment for  
                                        2020-2021 
Administration has reviewed the enrollment for elementary, middle, and high 
school attendance areas.  Due to the elementary and middle school  buildings’ 
total student enrollment and physical space constraints, we recommend that 
Barnard, B emis, Schroeder and Boulan Park  Schools be closed to open 
enrollment.  Our goal  for Athens and Troy High  has been to maximize each 
building’s enrollment which has resulted in  both schools being closed to open 
enrollment for the past several years. Based on current enrollment projections 
for the 2020-2021 school year, it is recommended that Athens and Troy High be 
closed to open enrollment with the exception of not more than (25) twenty-five 
incoming ninth grade students who reside in the Troy High School attendance 
area but wish to attend Athens High School.  Applications for the (25) twenty-
five spots at Athens High School will be accepted during a two-week window in 
March. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION: Adopt the following resolution. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by: __________________________ 
 Supported by: __________________________ 
Be it therefore resolved that the Troy Board of Education adopts a resolution to close 
Barnard, Bemis, Schroeder and Boulan Park Schools as well as Athens and Troy High 
Schools to open enrollment for the 2020-2021 school year, with the exception of not 
more than (25) twenty-five incoming ninth grade students who reside in the Troy High 
School attendance area but wish to attend Athens High School.  Students with siblings 
attending Athens and Troy High and currently enrolled in grades 9 – 11 will be allowed 
to open enroll to remain for the 2020-2021 school year.  
Yes: __________ 
No: __________

Projected Schools of ChoiceSibling Preference Placement forKindergarten, 1stGrade and Middle School Students 
9
A survey was emailed to current Schools of Choice families requesting information on siblingswho would be eligible for Kindergarten, 1stGrade and 6th, 7thand 8thGrade students for the 2020-2021 School Year.Number of Kindergarten students that plan to attend: 30Number of 1stGrade students that plan to attend: 1Number of Middle School students that plan to attend (6th, 7thand 8thcombined):13

10
DATE: February 11, 2020TO: TSD Board of EducationFROM: Kris Griffor, Asst. Supt., Elementary InstructionChristine DiPilato, Asst. Supt., Secondary  InstructionSUBJECT:   Schools of Choice Recommendations for the2020-2021 School YearThe following factors were considered in making a recommendation for a Schools of Choice Program for 2020-2021 school year:•Current enrollment across the Troy School District•Enrollment projections, patterns and trends•Numbers and percentages of Schools of Choice students currently in the district•Projected number of Schools of Choice siblings who would be age-eligible to enter the district in 2020-2021 if the decision is made to admit a “Limited” number of kindergarten, first and sixth through eighth grade students under Schools of Choice•Available elementary classroomsAfter analyzing the data and factors referenced above, we recommend that the district:•Use Plante Moran Cresa enrollment projection numbers as the foundation for determining the number of Schools of Choice (SOC) students to admit for the 2020-2021 school year. •Accept SOC applications for 30 Kindergarten, 1 First Grade and 13 Middle School students. Based upon the number of applications to kindergarten received for the 2019-2020 school year, we anticipate attracting many more than 50 applicants.  This would allow the District to admit 30 kindergarteners initially, with the option of admitting additional students, if space allows, in the third week of August.  •Middle school students would be placed in a building according to available space.

7. D.      02.25.20 
7. CURRICULUM 
D. CONSIDERATION:   Schools of Choice Section 105 & 105c  
In accordance with Michigan state law, school districts shall pass a resolution 
each school year if they intend to participate in a Schools of Choice program.  
It is anticipated that a limited number of seats will be available for the 2020-
2021 school year for kindergarten, first grade, and grades 6th - 8th at Baker, 
Larson and Smith  Middle Schools.  It would be appropriate at this time to 
consider adopting a resolution for Schools of Choice Section 105 and 105c for 
(30) thirty kindergarten, (1) one first grade, and ( 13) thirteen total 6th – 8th 
grade nonresident students who attend a school district within or outside of  
Oakland County , if that intermediate school district is contiguous with the 
Oakland Intermediate School District boundaries.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Adopt the following resolution. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by: __________________________ 
 Supported by: __________________________ 
Be it therefore resolved that the Board of Education of the Troy School 
District, exercising the option permitted by Section 105 & 105c of the State 
School Aid Act of 1979, as amended by Public Act 300 of 1996, will accept 
applications for a Limited Schools of Choice program for the 2020-2021 school 
year for (30) thirty kindergarten, (1) one first grade, and ( 13) thirteen total 
6th – 8th grade nonresident students who attend a school district within or 
outside of  Oakland County, if that intermediate school district is contiguous 
with the Oakland Intermediate School District boundaries.   
Yes: __________ 
No: __________

 TO:       Rick West - Asst. Superintendent - Business Services

 FROM:  Rob Carson – Director of Maintenance & Operations

RE:       RFP 9902 – Niles Relocation & Troy Union Lift Project    
1140 Rankin 
Troy, MI 48083 
Ph: 248.823.4050 
DATE:   February 6, 2020 
Fax: 248.823.4077 
RFP ( 9902) was issued  to obtain competitive bids for 
renovation work for the Niles Relocation Project and Troy Union 
Elementary Lift Project.  The scope of work for the Niles Relocation Project consists of a new 
office suite and building renovations consisting of flooring, painting, cabinetry, electrical, 
mechanical & door/access hardware.  The scope of work at Troy Union consists of creating an 
ADA lift between the original section and newer area of Troy Union Elementary.   A Bid 
Summary and Contract Award Recommendation from the Barton Malow Company is attached.     
The following vendors are recommended as low qualified bid for their category. 
Category                  Contractor       Amount 
021000 Demolition    DKI, Inc (Alt 1)       $     46,700.00 
060000 General Trades  Advanced Building Group      $   503,200.00 
096000 Flooring   Conventional Carpet (Alt 1)     $   177,900.00 
090000 Painting    General Painting       $     38,600.00 
123204 Casework/Millwork  FCI        $   160,000.00 
230000 Mechanical    Contrast Mechanical       $   189,000.00 
230900 Temperature Controls MCMI (BAS)       $     81,100.00 
260000 Electrical    Ecker (Alt. E2)       $   312,608.00 
     Total Contract Awards     $1,509,108.00 
       Contingency     $   135,940.50 
TOTAL AWARD VALUE   $1,645,048.50 
The cost for the Niles Relocation Project is $1,352,703.00 with a contingency cost of 
$120,300.00 for a total cost of $1,473,003.00.  The cost for the Troy Union Lift Project is 
$156,405.00 along with a contingency of $15,640.50 for a total project cost of $172,045.50. 
All proposals were reviewed and qualified by the Barton Malow Company.  A separate Purchase 
Order will be issued for each Bid Category.  The funding for these purchases will be from the 
Capitol Project Fund and the Niles Relocation Project Fund. 
If you have any questions, please let me know.  
Cc:  Purchasing Department – File

Troy School District 
Subject: Structured Cabling System RFP #9898 Recommendation Letter 
Beth, 
On January 13, 2020, Troy School District issued a Request for Proposal (RFP) soliciting bids for the Structured 
Cabling System for the former ITT Building.  The RFP was posted to the District’s website .    Convergent also spoke 
with several vendors regarding the solicitation and pointed them to the website to download the RFP and 
supporting documentation.  On January 27, 2020, sealed bids were accepted and opened by the District. Eight 
proposals were received.  The initial results were tabulated as follows: 
Contractor Base Bid 
AmComm $39,000.00 
Moss $39,896.89 
Conference Technologies $44,809.21 
AMR $47,563.00 
TTI $49,590.26 
Complete Interactive Technologies $52,237.00 
Trendset Communications $53,163.90 
Newkirk Electric $ 56,200.00 
All proposals were evaluated for compliance with the RFP requirements, pricing, technical solution and acceptance 
of terms and conditions of the RFP. With the exception of AmComm , MOSS and Newkirk Electric, all other bids 
were non-compliant with the RFP requirements and removed from consideration.   
During the initial evaluation it was noted that MOSS had included sales tax (they noted as such on the bid form as 
required). Once that cost was deducted from the base bid ($958.00) that brought their qualified price to 
$38,938.00 making them low bid. We proceeded with their evaluation of the bid response.  
It was found in the evaluation that they had provided additional material that was not needed for the project. In 
conversations with them and TSD IT it was agreed that these should be removed from the proposal. Deduct pricing 
was determined using the provided BOM with cost and associated labor identified by them: 
MOSS Base Bid $39,896.89 
Less Taxes identified <$958.00> 
Less Cable tray, brackets and 4 hours labor <$427.87> 
Less Horizontal 7 wire management panels <$566.81> 
Revised, qualified bid price $37,944.21 
MOSS is a well-established (40 years) and financially stable contractor with a very good industry reputation. They 
provided a complete and professional proposal. Our evaluation found their proposal to be complete for a 100% 
turnkey structured cabling system demo and reinstall in the former ITT building. 
141
Based on these factors, we recommend the award of the Structured Cabling System (RFP #9898) project to the low 
qualified bidder, MOSS, in the amount of $37,944.21, with an additional ~5% contingency of $1,897.00 for a total 
of $39,841.21.  Please contact me with any questions at 810-223-0531. 
Respectfully, 
Eric Helsel, RCDD/OSP/RTPM 
Project Manager, Convergent Technology Partners  
142

D. CONSIDERATION:  Flooring 
A RFP was issued to obtain competitive bids for flooring replacement 
and polishing work at Barnard, Costello, Hill, Martell and Wattles

School Bus Replacement Program - 2019-20 School Year  
Seven of the District’s bus fleet have reached their end-of-life cycle. Michigan School Business 
Officials, MSBO, offers a cooperative bus purchasing service to obtain competitive pricing 
from bidding dealers.

our District’s bus needs. After analyzing the information provided from three proposals and

          RE:     School Bus Purchase - Bond Funding                         
Date:  February 3, 2020 
To:     Rick West, Assistant Superintendent Business Services 
From:  Steve Coulter, School Products Manager, Hoekstra Transportation 
RE:  Fuel platforms available for school buses 
Rick, 
At your request I will provide a summary of fuel options for school buses today, 
and offer a business case to continue with clean diesel at Troy School District. We 
currently offer clean diesel, propane, CNG, and now a Battery electric EV model. 
We at Hoekstra Transportation feel strongly that clean diesel in the bridge to 
battery electric school buses, but the EV school bus must scale to lower the 
acquisition costs. We are involved in a pilot project to deliver the first EV school 
buses, the Thomas Built Jouley EV, to Ann Arbor and Roseville Schools. The 
project was directed by DTE, partnering with Thomas Built Buses, Proterra (our 
battery, traction drive and charging station supplier), along with our dealership. 
Applications were submitted to EGLE and each school was awarded grant funds 
through the Volkswagon Mitigation fund. This grant covered 70% of the cost of 
the bus, infrastructure and charging stations. The total cost came to 
approximately $360,000 per bus. 
As you can see, without a grant, the EV models are just not ready in price, and I 
might add, yet to be proven reliability and cost of operation – this is why we have 
pilot projects. We feel school bus operations are a great fit for EV because of the 
limited number of miles and predictable returns to the terminal for re-charging. If 
you think about your average mileage of 15,000 annually, at today’s fuel cost of 
around $2.20 per gallon of diesel and 7.5 mpg (conservatively), the total fuel  
costs over a 12 year life cycle would be $52,800. The EV will operate for less, not 
sure exactly how much in electricity, and will have a lower cost of operation – it 
just needs to scale, the cost of batteries will continue to go down, and we need 
proof of concept in terms of operational costs. Over the next decade, EV school 
buses will be a factor. 
173
CNG (compressed natural gas) is an option available on our product. CNG 
highlights include: 
 Product upcharge of approximately $35k. 
 Similar mpg to clean diesel. 
 Fuel is approximately 40% less than clean diesel. 
 Fast fill fueling station is $1-1.5M. 
Propane 
 Product upcharge of $8-10k. 
 Fuel is approximately 37% less money when including the federal .50 cent 
per gallon rebate that is available if you fuel on site, and is subject to 
renewal each federal budget year. 
 MPG is approximately 34% less than clean diesel, requiring more fossil fuel 
for similar distances. 
 Engine runs much hotter than diesel and longevity is a question we have.

Subject: AV Systems Upgrade RFP #9897 Recommendation Letter 
Beth, 
On December 11, 2019, Troy School District issued a Request for Proposal (RFP) soliciting bids for the Audio Video 
upgrade for the board room.  The RFP was posted to the District’s website .    Convergent also spoke with several 
vendors regarding the solicitation and pointed them to the website to download the RFP and supporting 
documentation.  On January 14, 2020, sealed bids were accepted and opened by the District. Six proposals were 
received.  The initial results were tabulated as follows: 
Contractor Base Bid 
Conference Technologies $51,543.37 
Sound Planning $57,467.04 
MOSS $60,379.45 
Advanced Lighting and Sound $62,345.00 
TEL Systems $68,893.00 
Conti Corporation $104,839.41 
All proposals were evaluated for compliance with the RFP requirements, pricing, technical solution and acceptance 
of terms and conditions of the RFP. Conference Technologies and Sound Planning Communications were both 
considered non-compliant and removed from consideration.  We proceeded with the evaluation of the qualified 
and compliant low bidder - MOSS. 
MOSS is a well-established and financially stable contractor with a very good industry reputation. They provided a 
complete and professional proposal. The core of their proposed system is a Crestron DMPS 350-C and a CP3 
processor, following the requirements and basis of design manufacturers in the RFP. Our evaluation found their 
proposal to be complete for a 100% turnkey AV upgrade to the boardroom. 
Based on these factors, we recommend the award of the AV Systems (RFP #9897) Upgrade project to the low 
qualified bidder, MOSS, in the amount of $60,379.45, with an additional ~5% contingency of $3,019.00 for a total 
of $63,398.45.  Please contact me with any questions at 810-223-0531. 
Respectfully, 
Eric Helsel, RCDD/OSP/RTPM 
Project Manager, Convergent Technology Partners

Board Room AV upgrade evaluation notes: Note the control processor in the design is also being used by 
the audio system that is not being replaced or upgraded 
Conference Technologies – 
1. Did not provide PLM Bond as required by RFP 
2. Did not provide cost analysis breakdown as required by RFP and required for evaluation 
3. Proposal valid for only sixty days, RFP required ninety days 
4. Did not provide new control processor (CP3) in their proposal as required by RFP, planned to 
reuse the old existing processor 
5. Did not include control of lights and shades in the scope of work of the programming 
6. Did not include iPad dock charging stations 
7. Did not include program monitor at broadcast console 
Sound Planning – 
1. Did not technically meet the requirements of the RFP: Proposed a network-based solution in lieu 
of the control processor as required by the RFP. The network switch they proposed would be a 
single point of failure or all audio and video equipment in the board room 
2. Did not include control of lights and shades in the scope of work of the programming 
3. Provided only one iPad dock charging station instead of the required two 
4. Preview monitor at broadcast console cut sheets show that it’s not rail mounted as required by 
the RFP

Dear Mr. Carson: 
As you are well aware, asbestos abatement work has to be performed as part of the renovation activities at Smith Middle 
School, Hill Elementary School, Larson Middle School, Boulan Park Middle School, Costello Elementary School, 
Wattles Elementary School, Barnard Elementary School and Athens High School .  The present project s involve the 
removal of flooring materials, pipe insulation, boilers and caulks. 
The following is Nova Environmental, Inc.’s recommendation regarding the selection of th e asbestos abatement 
Contractor for the Troy School District projects. 
BID NO. Buildings Contractor Bid Amount 
Larson Middle School, Boulan Park Middle School, 
Costello Elementary School, Wattles Elementary 
School, Barnard Elementary School and Athens High 
School 
This recommendation is based upon the following factors: 
1. The bid from the Contractor was the low bid. 
2. The Contractor has performed similar projects for numerous school districts. 
3. The Contractor  should be able to provide a safe and effective project within the time parameters of t he 
specification.  
For the above noted reasons, along with others, Nova recommends the bid proposal from the above noted Contractor be

8. J. 02.25.20 
8. BUSINESS 
J.  CONSIDERATION: iPad Purchases   
It is recommended that the following resolution to purchase iPads to be 
used for middle school testing be approved.  Pricing was obtained from 
REMC bid. 
 RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Accept the resolution below. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by:   
 Supported by: 
 Be it therefore resolved that the district purchase iPads and cases from 
Apple Computers in the amount of $367,500.00 and that these amounts be 
approved as an expenditure of the 2016 and 2019 Capital Projects Funds. 
 Yes: 
 No: 
186
Memorandum 
 TO:    Rick West, Assistant 
           Superintendent, Business Services 
Technology 
Services 
 FROM: Beth Soggs, Director of Technology 
4420 Livernois 
Phone: 248.823.5060 
 DATE: February 19, 2020 
When we began preparing for testing (WIDA & MSTEP) we verified that all devices 
in testing grade levels met the requirements set forth by DRC.  MDE was here 1 
week before WIDA testing started, and they checked all of our devices and 
confirmed they met the necessary hardware requirements.  Once testing began, we 
were having issues with WIDA ACCESS listening testing.  We reached out to DRC for 
support with the testing issues, and they stated that the iPad Air should not have 
been listed as an approved device since it does not have 2 GB of RAM.  They 
apologized for the error.   
This error on the part of DRC will also impact MSTEP testing for 7th and 8th grade 
students.  The ELA test for 7th grade and Science for 8th grade are the most 
concerning areas due to file sizes during the tests.  Students in grades 3 – 6 have 
newer iPad models that meet the 2 GB of RAM requirement.   
A solution to this problem would be to purchase the devices planned for June now 
so that we can put the devices on carts to deploy at the middle schools to be used 
by 7th and 8th grade students for testing.  Once testing is complete, the devices 
would be deployed to the current 7th grade students as part of the planned refresh 
cycle.   The only other solution would be to displace students from classes in the 
computer labs at the middle schools so the labs can be used for testing during the 
MSTEP window.  
We are asking the Board to approve the purchase of 1050 10.2” iPads with a 
Logitech keyboard case so that we can order and have the devices configured and 
ready for use by the April 13th.   The cost of this purchase will be $367,500 and is 
in line with the purchased planned for June from the technology bond. 
We apologize for the short notice of this request, but we were just made aware of 
the DRC error and feel this is the best way to reduce problems during the MSTEP 
testing period and prevents the disruption of instruction in the computer labs. 
187
 RE:  Middle School iPad Purchase 
Troy, MI 48098
